People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Hi, doctor. Thank you so much for seeing me. Um, I hope I'm not wasting your time with this, but... mostly I’m here because I’ve just been so tired lately. And it's, um, it's in a way that sleep doesn’t really seem to fix?

Um, well... I guess maybe a little? I mean, I know everyone gets in a rut sometimes, right? But, um, usually I really love doing things that make me feel like myself. Like, I normally do a lot of sketching, or I go to gallery openings... and I usually love cooking, doing yoga, or even just playing tennis with a friend. 

But lately, I just... I don't know, I guess I’ve kind of stopped caring about them as much? I feel terrible even saying that out loud, because I really don't want to sound lazy or anything. I am trying to keep up with my hobbies, I promise. It's just hard to find the interest right now. Is that... is that normal when you're just feeling really run down? I hope I'm explaining that right.

Um, well... I guess it's been a little while now. I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining, because I know everyone gets tired and has off days! But, um, this stretch where I've been feeling so worn out and just... not really interested in doing much... I think it's been going on for about three months?

I mean, I've really been trying to push through it and keep up with everything, I promise. It's just been a bit quieter lately, and the fatigue is kind of heavy. Oh, um... no, no, definitely not! I mean, I know I've been a little tired and slow lately, but I would never... I really value my life, I promise.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well... I wouldn't say I've *completely* lost interest, because I really do still like those things in theory! I just... I guess lately I've sort of stopped caring as much about the things that usually make me feel like myself. 

Like, usually I'm really into sketching, or going out to gallery openings. And I always try to keep up with cooking nice meals, doing my yoga, or even just playing tennis with a friend. But lately, I just... I haven't really been doing any of it. 

I don't want you to think I'm just being lazy or giving up, though! I really do plan to get back to all of it soon, I promise. It's just been a little hard to find the spark for them right now. Is it... is it okay that I'm taking a little break from those things? I hope that doesn't make me sound too boring or anything.

Um, well... I wouldn't say I feel down *most* of the day. I mean, I definitely have moments where I feel a little low, but I try really hard not to let it take over, you know?

Um, I know we just touched on this, and I... I hope I'm not repeating myself too much or being frustrating. But, um, no, I really wouldn't say I feel depressed most of the day.

Um, well... I wouldn't say I'm struggling, exactly. I mean, I definitely try to stay organized! I even have this great planning app on my phone to help keep me on track.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well... I wouldn't say they bring me *no* pleasure at all. I mean, I still think they're wonderful things to do! 

But, I guess... lately, I haven't really been keeping up with them as much as I usually do. Like, I usually love sketching, and going to gallery openings, or cooking a nice meal... even doing yoga or playing tennis with my friend. But recently, I just kind of... stopped doing them. 

It's not that I don't care about them anymore! I think I just haven't had the right mindset for it lately. I really hope that doesn't sound like I'm just giving up on my hobbies or being boring. I just haven't felt like myself enough to really get into them, if that makes sense? I'm definitely planning to get back to it soon, though! Is that the kind of thing you were asking about?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um, well... I definitely wouldn't say I feel down most of the day. I mean, I try really hard to keep a positive attitude! I think it's so important to focus on the bright side of things. 

I do have moments where I feel a little... flat, maybe? But I wouldn't call it depressed. I just think everyone has days where they aren't feeling one hundred percent, right? I really hope that doesn't make me sound like I'm complaining or being dramatic. I'm generally a very cheerful person!

I think it's mostly just because I've been so tired, you know, it's hard to muster up the energy to make a full meal. I really am trying to eat, I promise. Is that... does that make sense for what you're asking?\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling worthless or guilty, even about things that normally wouldn’t bother you?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I definitely don't walk around feeling worthless or anything like that! I always try to see the value in myself and, you know, look on the bright side. \n\nI guess sometimes if I don't get everything done that I planned, like with the chores or my classwork, I might feel a tiny bit bad about it? But I think that's just normal, right? Like, anyone would feel a little guilty if they were falling behind. It's not like a deep, heavy guilt or anything. I just want to make sure I'm being a good partner and keeping up with my responsibilities. \n\nSo, no, I wouldn't say I feel worthless at all. I hope that's the right kind of answer? I just want to be as helpful as I can.\n\nDoctor: Have you had trouble concentrating on tasks, such as reading, following conversations, or making decisions, even when you're not physically tired?\n\nPatient: Um, well... maybe just a little bit? I mean, I really try to stay organized, I promise. I use this planning app to keep everything on track because I want to make sure I'm staying on top of things. \n\nBut, um... lately, I guess I have had a little trouble with making decisions? Like, I'll sit there with my app open, and I just... I can't figure out if I should work on a class assignment, or pay a bill, or go start dinner. My brain just sort of stalls, and I end up sitting there doing none of it for a little while. \n\nI feel really silly even admitting that, because I don't want you to think I'm just sitting around being lazy! I really do want to get it all done. It's just hard to focus my thoughts sometimes to pick a starting point. Does that make sense? I hope that's the kind of example you were looking for.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way? Has it been going on for weeks, months, or longer?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I guess it's been a little while now. I really don't want to sound like I'm complaining, because I know everyone gets tired and has off days! But, um, this stretch where I've been feeling so worn out and just... not really interested in doing much... I think it's been going on for about three months?\n\nI mean, I've really been trying to push through it and keep up with everything, I promise. It's just been a bit quieter lately, and the fatigue is kind of heavy. But three months sounds about right, I think. Is that... is that a normal amount of time for something like this? I just want to make sure I'm giving you the right timeline.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or on the flip side, feeling restless or fidgety in a way that's unusual for you?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I definitely don't think I'm restless or anything like that. I'm usually pretty calm, I think! \n\nBut, um... I guess maybe on my slower days, I might be a little bit sluggish? I don't want to make it sound worse than it is, because I really do try my best to keep up. But sometimes it just feels like... like my body is moving through wet cement, if that makes sense? \n\nAnd, um, my spouse has pointed out that sometimes it takes me a weirdly long time just to answer a simple question. I feel so bad about it, because I don't want them to think I'm ignoring them! I really am listening. It just takes my brain a minute to put the words together and get them out. \n\nI hope that doesn't make me sound too terrible. I really am trying to be more responsive. Is that the kind of thing you meant?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt so down or overwhelmed that you've thought about harming yourself or wished you weren't here?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, no, definitely not! I mean, I know I've been a little tired and slow lately, but I would never... I really value my life, I promise. \n\nI mean, sure, sometimes when I'm feeling really overwhelmed with trying to get everything done, I might think, like, \"Oh, I just want to crawl under the covers and sleep for a week.\" But that's just because I'm tired, you know? It's not because I don't want to be here. I really want to get better and get back to being helpful around the house and everything. \n\nI hope you don't think I'm... I don't know, unstable or anything just because I've been having a hard time. I really am trying my best to stay positive. Does that make sense? I just want to reassure you that I'm perfectly safe, really.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve completely lost interest in most or all activities, even ones you used to really enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I wouldn't say I've *completely* lost interest, because I really do still like those things in theory! I just... I guess lately I've sort of stopped caring as much about the things that usually make me feel like myself. \n\nLike, usually I'm really into sketching, or going out to gallery openings. And I always try to keep up with cooking nice meals, doing my yoga, or even just playing tennis with a friend. But lately, I just... I haven't really been doing any of it. \n\nI don't want you to think I'm just being lazy or giving up, though! I really do plan to get back to all of it soon, I promise. I hope that doesn't make me sound too boring or anything.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that you’ve experienced a significant loss of interest or pleasure in almost all activities, even ones you used to really enjoy?\n\nPatient: Um, I know we just talked about this a little bit, and I...
